## 2026-10-15 (day 3) — Brain / Architect (claude/opus, effort high) — re-review of T-030-R1 (`d53f9b4`)

- **Run:** `255f268e-d039-425b-88b2-427791553746`; log `.tasks/logs/255f268e-d039-425b-88b2-427791553746.log`; spec role `brain`, action `review`, base `ec740ab`, write scope this file only. The launcher writes `.tasks/receipts/255f268e-….json` when this run ends; it did not exist while this entry was written.
- **Run under review (receipt re-checked):** `88d7ef67-912d-429e-9885-09a153cfb81c` (T-030-R1, codex.economy `gpt-6-luna`): completed, exit 0, `workspace_changed: true`, scope pass, no violations, `e12bb6a` → `d53f9b4` clean, 3 paths, all in the allow-list. VerificationRecord `88d7ef67….json` result `pass`.
- **Test conditions:** `git archive` export of `d53f9b4` in the session scratchpad; fake `claude`, `codex`, `cursor-agent`, `agy`, `agent`, `grok` (exit 97) first on PATH; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. Each hostile case ran in its own temporary Git repo with a copy of `scripts/ledger.py` and `tests/fixtures/ledger/good-completed.json`. The secret-shaped probe value was built at run time and never written to a repo file. No real provider CLI was launched. Live tree afterwards: `git status` empty, no `__pycache__`, no `docs/missions/ledger.jsonl`, `.tasks/ledger/` untouched by this run.
- **(1) Exactly once per `run_id`: fixed.** Same `run_id` in a second file → exit 1 (`row.run_id: must match file name`), nothing written. Renamed row → exit 1 on every fold, never appended. Committed `run_id` with different content → exit 1, `ledger.jsonl` byte-identical. Identical content → `rows=0`. In-batch duplicate drift → `drift=1`. Fold, then new row + new drift, fold again → old bytes are a prefix of both files.
- **(2) Drift validation, hand-built 6-key events: works.** Secret in `to.model` → `drift.to.model: privacy`, value absent from stdout/stderr, no `drift.jsonl` written. Extra top-level key with a home path → `unknown key; name not printed`. Absolute path in `seat` → `privacy`. Non-object → `must be an object`. Uppercase `inventory_sha256` → refused. Other mission's event → skipped. **See BLOCKING 1 and 3.**
- **(3) Non-object row, non-object committed line, committed line without `run_id`:** each `LedgerError`, exit 1, no traceback.
- **(4) Files:** FIFO row → exit 1 at once (`not a regular file`), no hang. Symlinked row → `symlink refused`. 200 MB sparse row → `file exceeds size limit`. Symlinked `ledger.jsonl` and `drift.jsonl` targets → refused, the outside file stays empty. Errors name the file only when its stem is UUID-shaped. **See BLOCKING 2.**
- **(5) Mutations (scratch copies; tree's `tests/test-ledger.py` + integration `-k test_ledger_fold`): 13 of 19 caught.** Caught: `ledger.jsonl` `"w"`, drift mission filter removed, non-canonical row line, stem check removed, conflicting duplicate allowed, in-batch drift dedupe removed, `validate_drift` call removed, row non-object guard removed, committed-line `run_id` check removed, `safe_read` follows symlinks (`lstat`→`stat`), regular-file check removed, size checks removed, drift privacy walk removed. **Survived:** `drift.jsonl` opened `"w"` (real gap: no test folds into an existing non-empty `drift.jsonl`); `from`/`to` resolution-shape check removed (untested); `inventory_sha256` check removed (untested); drift non-object guard, committed non-object guard, target symlink check (all three redundant with another guard, so equivalent mutants).
- **Audit:** `bash scripts/verify-mission.sh docs/missions/20261014-2a-foundation` → `VERIFY OK`, exit 0, before this entry.
- **Not done:** no `accept-feature.sh`; no features.json, fleet, gate or status change; no worker dispatched; no task file written.
- **BLOCKING 1: the fold refuses every real drift event, and then folds nothing.** `validate_drift` requires 6 keys in `from`/`to` (`provider`, `door`, `model`, `effort`, `speed`, `source`). `fleet_state.py resolve` passes `drift()` a 4-key result (`provider`, `model`, `effort`, `speed`), and `from` is either `approved_resolution` (the same 4 keys) or the previous event's `to`. So every real event has 4-key `from`/`to`. Proven end to end: real `spawn-worker.sh` run with a fake codex, inventory change, second run writes the drift line; `ledger.py fold` → exit 1, `ERROR: ledger: drift.from.door: is required`, and the two valid rows in `.tasks/ledger/` are not folded either. One drift event blocks the mission's whole ledger. The test fixture `good_drift()` is hand-built with 6 keys, so no test sees the real shape. The ambiguity started in my T-030 review ("the resolution keys"); this is the decision: `from`/`to` have exactly `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `speed`, each a non-empty string.
- **BLOCKING 2: the 1 MiB read cap also applies to the committed `docs/missions/ledger.jsonl` (and `drift.jsonl`).** A row is about 1.6 KB, so at 647 rows every fold fails with `ledger.jsonl: file exceeds size limit`, permanently. The file is repo-wide and append-only, and `.tasks/receipts/` already holds 139 runs. The cap is right for `.tasks/` inputs; the committed targets need only the symlink/regular-file checks.
- **BLOCKING 3: a refusal can print a secret-shaped key name.** `check_drift_strings` walks `from`/`to` with raw key names before their key set is checked. A drift event with `from` = {<secret-shaped key>: <home path>} gives `ERROR: ledger: drift.from.<the secret>: privacy` on stderr. Nothing is written (the fold stops), but the refusal message carries the value-like key. With a benign value the same key gives `unknown key; name not printed`, so the order of checks is the bug.
- **Required follow-up `T-030-R2`** (F005; `scripts/ledger.py`, `tests/test-ledger.py`, `tests/test-execution-integration.py`, PROGRESS.md; economy seat):
  1. `from`/`to` keys exactly `provider`, `model`, `effort`, `speed` (non-empty strings). Check both shapes (`check_object`) before any privacy walk, so no unknown key name is ever printed. Change `good_drift()` to that shape.
  2. Apply `MAX_INPUT_BYTES` only to `.tasks/ledger/*.json` and `.tasks/drift/<slug>.jsonl`; read the committed `ledger.jsonl`/`drift.jsonl` without a size cap (keep the symlink/regular-file refusals). Test: a committed `ledger.jsonl` larger than 1 MiB still folds.
  3. Tests that kill the survivors: fold drift, add a new event, fold again, old `drift.jsonl` bytes are a prefix (kills drift `"w"`); a missing resolution key and an extra resolution key are refused; a bad `inventory_sha256` is refused; a secret-shaped nested key name is absent from stdout and stderr.
  4. Integration: extend the existing drift test (real wrapper, fake provider, inventory change) with `ledger.py fold` → exit 0, `rows=2 drift=1`; then a second fold → `rows=0 drift=0`.
  - Review: items 1–2 are evidence/privacy code. A short Architect check of the four items, or `code-reviewer` plus the integration test above, is proportional.
- **Informational, owner choice (not blocking):** a symlinked `.tasks/ledger/` directory is followed (rows inside an outside directory are folded); any non-JSON file in `.tasks/ledger/`, of any mission, stops every fold (fails closed). The T-030 review's open owner decisions (spec-supplied fields labelled `observed`; meaning of `door`) are still open and still must be settled before T-032's first real fold.

Verdict: FAIL. Three blocking issues: (1) every real drift event (4-key `from`/`to`, as `fleet_state.py` writes it) is refused, and the refusal stops the whole fold, rows included; (2) the 1 MiB cap on the committed `ledger.jsonl` makes every fold fail after about 647 rows; (3) a secret-shaped nested key name is printed in a refusal. The T-030 blocking issues 1 (exactly once per `run_id`) and 2 (drift privacy for well-formed events) are fixed; issue 3 is fixed for `ledger.jsonl` but not for `drift.jsonl`. The rest of R1 (file-type hardening, run_id identity, no tracebacks) is sound and does not need to be redone.

- **Run under review (receipt re-checked):** `1481a3cf-f301-45aa-8ad9-8fa93ca82b89` (T-030, cursor.economy): completed, exit 0, `workspace_changed: true`, scope pass, no violations, `d81d645` → `451aa2c` clean, 4 paths, all in the allow-list.
- **Test conditions:** fake `cursor-agent`, `codex`, `claude`, `agy`, `agent`, `grok` (exit 97) first on PATH; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`; a `git archive` export of `451aa2c` in the session scratchpad. Every fold probe ran in its own temporary Git repo with a copy of `scripts/ledger.py` and the tree's `good-completed.json` fixture. No real provider CLI was launched. The secret-shaped probe value was built at run time and never written to a repo file. Baseline at `451aa2c`: `test-ledger.py` 47 OK; integration `test_ledger_fold_*` OK. Live tree afterwards: `git status` empty, `.tasks/ledger/` unchanged (7 rows), no `docs/missions/ledger.jsonl`, no `__pycache__`.
- **(1) Once, in order, canonical, second fold is a no-op: confirmed only for well-formed input.** Rows are sorted by `run_id` and written as `sort_keys` compact lines. A second fold prints `folded rows=0 drift=0` and changes nothing. A crash after the first of 3 rows (an `os._exit` mutant) leaves 1 complete line. The rerun appends the other 2, with 3 unique ids, still in order. **See BLOCKING 1.**
- **(2) Validation before append; refused value never printed: confirmed for rows.** Every matching row is validated before anything is written. One refused row stops the whole fold (exit 1, nothing appended). A secret in `requested_model.value` gives `ERROR: ledger: requested_model.value: privacy`, and the value is not in stdout or stderr. Read-only `ledger.jsonl`: exit 1, size still 0. Read-only mission directory: the rows are appended and the drift write fails (exit 1). After the fix, the rerun gives `rows=0 drift=1`, which is a safe recovery. **Not atomic:** I appended a half line by hand to simulate a crash mid-write. Every later fold then fails (`ERROR: ledger: Unterminated string…`, exit 1). It fails closed and `git restore` repairs it, because the file is tracked.
- **(3) Hostile files in `.tasks/ledger/`:** another mission's row is skipped. **But** a non-JSON file of *any* mission (or a symlink to `/etc/passwd`) stops every fold, and the message does not name the file. A row that is a JSON list, a drift line that is a list, or an existing `ledger.jsonl` line without `run_id` each end with an uncaught traceback (exit 1, no value printed). A symlink to a valid row outside the repo is followed and folded. A FIFO named `<uuid>.json` hangs the fold (killed at 5 s). A 200 MB file is read into memory whole. A `docs/missions/ledger.jsonl` that is a symlink is followed, and the row is written **outside the repo**. Only the two target files change in the normal case (hash walk of the temp repo).
- **(4) Drift:** dedupe by sha256 against the committed file works across folds. **See BLOCKING 2.** The mission filter compares the full path (`fleet_state.py drift()` writes `fleet["mission"]["path"]`). Rows compare the slug (the runner writes `Path(spec["mission"]).name`). Each matches its writer.
- **(5) Integration test:** confirmed by reading it and running it. It covers a real wrapper run with a fake provider, the `clean_workspace` preflight, `verify-mission.sh --run-id`, fold + `git add docs/missions` + commit, a second fold with `rows=0 drift=0`, and empty `git status`. It covers no drift.
- **(6) Mutations (scratch copies; tree's `test-ledger.py` + integration `test_ledger_fold`): 8 of 12 caught.** Caught: once-only check removed, order reversed, sort removed, validation skipped, row mission filter removed, drift dedupe removed, drift not written, write before validate. **Survived:** drift mission filter removed; `ledger.jsonl` opened `"w"` instead of `"a"` (**wipes all previously committed rows**); `drift.jsonl` opened `"w"`; row line not canonical (`json.dumps(row)`).
- **Audit:** `bash scripts/verify-mission.sh docs/missions/20261014-2a-foundation` → `VERIFY OK`, exit 0, before this entry.
- **Not done:** no `accept-feature.sh`; no features.json, fleet, gate or status change; no worker dispatched; no task file written.
- **BLOCKING 1: exactly once per `run_id` is not enforced.** The fold keys on the file name, not on the row's `run_id`, and does not dedupe inside one batch. Shown: (a) a second file with the same `run_id` and different content (`feature` F002) is appended, so `ledger.jsonl` has the same `run_id` twice; (b) two such files in one batch: `rows=2`; (c) a row whose file name is not its `run_id` is appended again on **every** fold (`rows=1`, then `rows=1`, giving 2 lines), so the second fold is not a no-op. `.tasks/ledger/` is gitignored and outside the receipt scope check, so any worker can create these files. The same in-batch gap exists for drift: the same event twice in the source gives `drift=2`.
- **BLOCKING 2: drift events go into a committed file unvalidated.** Any JSON line in `.tasks/drift/<slug>.jsonl` whose `mission` matches is copied into `<mission>/drift.jsonl` (tracked). Shown: a line with a secret-shaped `to.model`, an extra key holding an absolute home path, and no schema check was folded (`drift=1`, committed file contains the secret). This is the same kind of leak the ledger privacy rule exists to stop. A push-protection block has already happened once on this branch.
- **BLOCKING 3: the tests do not catch a fold that destroys committed history.** The `"w"`-instead-of-`"a"` mutant passes every test, because no test folds new rows into an existing non-empty `ledger.jsonl` or `drift.jsonl`. The drift mission filter is also untested.
- **Required follow-up `T-030-R1`** (F005; `scripts/ledger.py`, `tests/test-ledger.py`, PROGRESS.md; economy seat):
  1. Key rows on `row["run_id"]`. Refuse a row whose file stem ≠ its `run_id`. Refuse a `run_id` already in `ledger.jsonl` or earlier in the batch, unless its canonical line is byte-identical (then skip it). Dedupe drift inside the batch too.
  2. Validate each drift event before append. It must be an object with exactly the keys `drift()` in `scripts/fleet_state.py` writes (`at`, `mission`, `seat`, `from`, `to`, `inventory_sha256`). `from`/`to` must be objects with the resolution keys. `inventory_sha256` must be 64 lowercase hex. Every string goes through the ledger's free-text privacy check. Refusals name the field, never the value.
  3. A non-object row or drift line, and a `ledger.jsonl` line without `run_id`, raise `LedgerError` (exit 1, no traceback).
  4. Tests that kill the four survivors. Fold, add a new row and a new drift event, fold again: the old lines are byte-identical and the new ones are appended. Another mission's drift is skipped. Each written row equals `canonical_row_line`. Add negatives for 1–3 (same `run_id` in two files; stem mismatch; secret, unknown key and absolute path in drift; value absent from stdout and stderr).
  - Recommended, same task if small: read only regular, non-symlink files with a size cap (this fixes the FIFO hang, symlink-follow and huge-file cases); refuse a symlinked target file; name the offending file in errors only when its stem is UUID-shaped.
  - Review: items 1–2 are privacy/evidence code. A short Architect re-review limited to the hostile cases above, plus `code-reviewer`, is proportional.
- **Owner decisions still open, and now urgent:** the T-029 review's (3) source labels for spec-supplied fields and (3b) the meaning of `door` were to be "settled before T-030 commits any row". Neither is recorded as decided, and neither T-031 nor T-032 addresses them. All 7 live rows carry `door` = tier (`economy`/`unknown`) and `effort` with `source: observed` taken from the spec. `ledger.jsonl` is append-only, so T-032's first real fold makes these values permanent. Decide before T-032 runs.
- **Informational, owner choice:** the fold does not check that a row's `run_id` has a receipt in `.tasks/receipts/`, so a made-up row with a fresh UUID that passes validation is committed. `normalize_mission_path` strips the leading `/` before testing for it, so `/abs/path` is accepted as relative. The fold does not check that the mission folder exists.

Verdict: FAIL. Three blocking issues: (1) exactly-once per `run_id` is keyed on the file name, so a duplicate or renamed row is committed again and again; (2) drift events are committed with no schema or privacy check; (3) no test catches a fold that truncates `ledger.jsonl` or `drift.jsonl`. The fold's structure (sorted, validate-all-then-append, idempotent across folds, rows of other missions filtered) is sound and does not need to be redone.

- **Run:** `808ef7ff-4dc2-416a-a5ae-750c8968870f`; log `.tasks/logs/808ef7ff-4dc2-416a-a5ae-750c8968870f.log`; spec role `brain`, action `review`, base `3d87ae9`, write scope this file only. The launcher writes `.tasks/receipts/808ef7ff-….json` when this run ends; it did not exist while this entry was written.
- **Runs under review (receipts re-checked):** `0f894102-…` (T-028-R4, codex.economy): completed, exit 0, `workspace_changed: true`, scope pass, `3ff531b` → `0ad14ec` clean, 3 paths in the allow-list. `5612630d-…` (T-029-R1, cursor.economy): completed, exit 0, `workspace_changed: true`, scope pass, `9443e90` → `d92e122` clean, 3 paths in the allow-list. Both VerificationRecords exist.
- **Test conditions:** fake `cursor-agent`, `codex`, `claude`, `agy`, `agent`, `grok` (exit 97) first on PATH; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`; `git archive` exports of both commits in the session scratchpad. Runner scenarios used the tree's own `WrapperIntegration` fixture (temporary repo, stub providers). No real provider CLI was launched. The live tree is clean afterwards; `.tasks/ledger/` still holds only the 3 rows written by earlier real runs (all 3 validate with the new `ledger.py`); no `__pycache__`.
- **(A) `0ad14ec`, privacy rule.** 17 required values (`/tmp/x`, `/etc/passwd`, `~/notes`, `$HOME/x`, `bearer`/`BEARER`/tab/mixed-case-tab bearer, each of U+202A–202E and U+2066–2069) placed in all 20 free-text and nested locations of `hostile_locations()`: 340 of 340 refused. The CLI exits non-zero and never prints the value (17 of 17). Legitimate values pass: `scripts/ledger.py`, `tests/test-ledger.py`, `docs/missions/x/tasks/T-028.md`, `./scripts/x.sh`, `bash#1`, `tmp/x`, `etc/x`, `a~b`, `cost$5`, bare `bearer`. **Mutations (scratch copies, tree's `test-ledger.py`): 12 of 12 caught**, each by its own test. The 12: whole sensitive-path check removed; `/tmp/`, `/etc/`, `~/` and `$HOME` each removed alone; each bidi range removed; U+202E alone and U+2069 alone dropped; bearer made case-sensitive; bearer space-only; bearer rule removed.
- **(A) informational, not blocking:** these near-miss spellings are still accepted: `/tmp` and `/etc` without the trailing slash, `/TMP/x`, `${HOME}/x`, `~root/x`, `Bearer` followed by two spaces, NBSP or a colon, and U+061C. `/var/tmp/x` is refused, but only because it contains `/tmp/`. Extending the rule to cover these is an owner decision.
- **(B) `d92e122`, runner.** **Confirmed:** build, validate and write sit in one `try` with `LedgerError` → `OSError` → `Exception`. A receipt read error is now separate from ledger errors. The receipt is written before the ledger step and the ledger step never touches it. The `timed_out` and `launch_failed` rows exist.
- **(B) BLOCKING 1: a broken `scripts/ledger.py` still changes the run's outcome.** `from ledger import …` runs *after* the provider exits and reads the working tree's file, but only `ImportError` is caught. End-to-end, with a provider that exits 0 after editing `scripts/ledger.py` (a normal slip on any ledger task; T-028-R4's allow-list had this file):
  - `SyntaxError` or `NameError` at import: spawn exit **1**, traceback.
  - `RuntimeError` or `ValueError` at import: spawn exit **125**, with `ERROR: terminal execution evidence failed`.
  - In all four cases the receipt says completed, exit 0, but there is no `receipt=` line and no WARN. This breaks objective 1 ("catch any `Exception` around the whole ledger step … leave the exit code, the `receipt=` line … exactly as without the ledger").
  - A deleted `ledger.py` is handled correctly: WARN `module unavailable`, exit 0.
  - Separately, a `validate_row` that calls `sys.exit(0)` drops the `receipt=` line. That needs deliberately hostile code, so it is informational.
  - **Fix:** catch `Exception` around the import as well. Better: import `ledger` at runner start, before the provider launches, inside `try/except Exception`. Then code a worker edited is never run in the runner after the run.
- **(B) BLOCKING 2: test (f) does not catch the clobber fault on its own.** Its live second-write probe sets `observed_model.value = 'mutated'` while the source stays `unknown`. The validator refuses that row (`observed_model.value: must be null when source is unknown`), so the probe never reaches the write. The embedded mutant only proves anything because it *also* removes `validate_row`.
  - A clobber-only mutant (`os.link` → `os.replace`, validation kept) **survives the whole integration file** (exit 0).
  - A valid second row (receipt `feature = 'F002'`) does overwrite the row under that mutant (bytes changed). It leaves the row unchanged under `d92e122` (WARN `write failed`).
  - Clobber was one of the six faults the T-029 review required tests to catch.
- **(B) other mutants (scratch copies, full `test-execution-integration.py`, failures counted only when a behavioural assertion failed, not a pattern-not-found):** 13 of 15 caught: WARN printing the row; narrowed catch; validation skipped; observed model from the spec; file count forced to 0; tokens swapped (both directions); label index dropped; occurrence never counted; write failure raising; `LedgerError` escaping; ledger step skipped; WARN turned into a non-zero exit. **Informational (caught by pattern only):** the generic `except Exception` WARN printing `{exc!r}` is caught only because a test's own mutation pattern is missing. No test proves that the generic WARN never prints exception text. Today's code does not print it.
- **Audit:** `bash scripts/verify-mission.sh docs/missions/20261014-2a-foundation` → `VERIFY OK`, exit 0, before this entry.
- **Not done:** no `accept-feature.sh`; no features.json, fleet, gate or status change; no worker dispatched; no task file written.
- **Required follow-up `T-029-R2`** (F005; `scripts/execution_runner.py`, `tests/test-execution-integration.py`, PROGRESS.md; economy seat):
  1. Make the `ledger` import part of the guarded ledger step: catch `Exception`, and preferably import before provider launch.
  2. Integration test: a provider that leaves `scripts/ledger.py` with a `SyntaxError`, and one that raises `RuntimeError` at import. Each must keep exit 0, the `receipt=` line and a WARN. Mutation: restoring `except ImportError` alone must fail the test.
  3. In test (f), the live second write must use a row that validates (for example receipt `feature = 'F002'`). The embedded mutant must be `os.replace` alone, with validation kept.
  4. Optional: a test that the generic WARN never contains exception text.
  - `code-reviewer`, plus re-running these two end-to-end scenarios, is enough. No full Architect pass is needed.

Verdict (T-028-R4, `0ad14ec`): PASS.

Verdict (T-029-R1, `d92e122`): FAIL. Two blocking issues: (1) an unimportable `scripts/ledger.py` left by a worker changes the spawn exit (1 or 125) and drops the `receipt=` line and the WARN; (2) test (f) does not catch the clobber fault on its own, so a clobber-only mutant survives. Everything else in the change is sound and does not need to be redone.

Receipt (re-checked): completed, exit 0, `workspace_changed: true`, scope pass, `7efefe6` → `b1fb35b` clean, 3 paths in the allow-list. The diff to the runner only adds code: new functions, one import change, and one block after `write_receipt`.
- **Test conditions:** fake `cursor-agent`, `codex`, `claude`, `agy`, `agent`, `grok` (exit 97) first on PATH; `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`; `git archive` exports of `7efefe6` (old) and `b1fb35b` (new) in the session scratchpad. Every run used the tree's own integration fixture (temporary repo, stub providers). The launch-failure case ran with a PATH that holds no provider CLI. No real provider CLI was launched. The live tree is clean afterwards and has no `.tasks/ledger/`.
- **(1) Ledger problems vs run outcome: partly confirmed; one gap (blocking).** I ran 16 scenarios on the old and the new tree. The spawn exit code was the same in all of them. The receipt was valid and equal except for per-run fields (run id, times, `argv_sha256` of the temporary path). Cases: read-only `.tasks/ledger`, which logs `WARN: ledger row not written: ledger: write failed`. `.tasks/ledger` as a regular file, and as a symlink to an outside directory, each log a WARN; nothing was written outside the repo. When the validator refuses a hostile pool value, the WARN reads `pool: privacy`, and the value is not in the log, stdout or stderr. **Gap:** the runner catches only `OSError`, `JSONDecodeError` and `SpecError` around the ledger step. If the worker leaves `harness-registry.json` as a JSON list, `registry_pool_and_billing` raises `AttributeError` *after* the receipt is written. The runner then exits 1 instead of 0, with a traceback, no `receipt=` line and no WARN; the log's `exit=` line says 1 while the receipt says 0. Today the spawn's final exit code is 1 in both trees, because `dispatch_policy.py release-lock` also fails on that registry, so the effect is masked. It still breaks T-029 objective 2 ("receipt, outcome and exit code unchanged"). Any `ValueError` there would also become exit 125 through `main()`. No test covers objective 2's WARN path.
- **(2) Per outcome:** a row is written and validates for `completed`, `failed` (7), `timed_out` (124), `interrupted` (SIGTERM, 143) and `launch_failed` (127, bad interpreter, no CLI on PATH). Nothing is written when the runner is SIGKILLed (no receipt either; spawn exits 125), when it refuses before launch (spec hash, log path), or when `write_receipt` fails. In all three cases there is no receipt, so there is nothing to summarise.
- **(3) Spec values labelled `observed`: not confirmed as asked, but not a T-029 defect.** `observed_model`, the four token counts and `fast_state` copy the receipt's `observation` value and source unchanged. `cost` and `quota_share` are `unknown`. `requested_model`, `resolved_model` and `effort` come from the **spec** and are marked `source: observed`. In all 31 real current receipts, the row's `effort` is `observed` while the receipt's `observed_configuration.effort` is `unknown`. The schema allows only observed, estimated or unknown, so the worker had no correct label. This is a T-027 schema decision; decide it before T-030 folds anything. My recommendation: take `resolved_model` and `effort` from the receipt's `resolved_configuration` (same values in every case I replayed), and state in `ledger.py` that "observed" for requested or resolved fields means "recorded by the harness".
- **(3b) `door` holds the tier** (`economy`; `unknown` for brain runs). This follows the T-027 fixtures, but proposal §5 defines a door as a CLI plus its subscription, and the registry names them (`cursor-composer`, `cursor-grok`, …). The pool lookup maps cursor to `cursor-grok` only when `speed == "fast"`, so Grok at standard speed is counted as Composer. The pool value is right today only because both cursor doors share the pool `cursor`. Pool and billing class are read from the working tree *after* the run, so a worker can rewrite its own row's `billing_class` (shown: `rewritten-by-worker`, row valid). Read them at `base_commit` instead. Not blocking for T-029; it should be fixed before F005 acceptance.
- **(4) Real data replay (read-only):** 31 of 31 current receipts give a valid row, with 0 privacy refusals. 83 older receipts (v1 or legacy, no `observation`) raise `KeyError`; new runs cannot hit this, because `write_receipt` always writes `observation`. Labels: all 399 real verification commands across all mission tasks get a label that passes, and none is longer than 40 characters. 24 of them would be refused if written in full. The runner itself writes `checks: []`, because the checks run later at verification, so the label function is not used yet (it is meant for T-030/T-031).
- **(5) Atomic, no clobber, ignored:** two runs give two rows, keyed by their receipt ids, mode `0400`, with no `.tmp` files left over. The no-clobber path is `os.link`. `git status --porcelain` is empty after every scenario except the worker-rewrites-registry cases, where the worker itself changed `harness-registry.json`. `git add -A --dry-run` never lists `.tasks/`.
- **(6) Other runner behaviour:** confirmed by reading the diff (only additions; the ledger step runs after `write_receipt`; gates, lock, spec hash binding and exit-code mapping are untouched). The old/new scenario comparison above agrees.
- **(7) Mutations: NOT FINISHED.** I made 15 mutant copies (no ledger call, forced outcome, clobber, `LedgerError` escaping, the WARN printing the row, verbatim label, row written into a tracked directory, observed model taken from the spec, diff files forced to 0, write failure raising, outer `except` narrowed, success-only rows, swapped tokens, validation skipped, label without index). **Result (added after the run finished, full integration suite and `test-ledger.py` for each mutant): 5 caught, 10 survived.** Caught: no ledger call, forced outcome, verbatim label, row written into a tracked directory, success-only rows. **Survived:** clobber (`os.replace`), `LedgerError` escaping, the WARN printing the row, observed model taken from the spec, diff files forced to 0, write failure raising, outer `except` narrowed, swapped tokens, validation skipped, label without index. T-029-R1 must add tests that catch at least these: clobber, the WARN and `LedgerError` paths, write failure raising, outer `except` narrowed, and validation skipped.
- **Audit:** `bash scripts/verify-mission.sh docs/missions/20261014-2a-foundation` → `VERIFY OK`, exit 0, before this entry.
- **Not done:** no `accept-feature.sh`; no features.json, fleet, gate or status change; no worker dispatched; no task file written.
- **Required follow-up `T-029-R1`** (F005; `scripts/execution_runner.py`, `tests/test-execution-integration.py`, PROGRESS.md; economy seat; `code-reviewer` review is enough): (1) the ledger step must catch any `Exception`, log `WARN: ledger row not written: <field or stage>` and never change the exit code or the `receipt=` line; (2) integration tests, built in a temporary repo, for read-only `.tasks/ledger`, a validator refusal (the WARN names the field, and the value appears nowhere) and a registry the worker made malformed: exit code and receipt the same as the provider's, WARN present; (3) rows for `timed_out` and `launch_failed` in the existing test; (4) a second write with the same run id must not replace the first row. Optional, if the owner agrees: read pool and billing class at `base_commit`.
- **Owner decisions (not blocking T-029):** (3) the source labels for spec-supplied fields, and (3b) the meaning of `door`. Both should be settled before T-030 commits any row.

## 2026-10-15 (night 2) — Front Desk (Sonnet 5.5) — T-028-R1 code-reviewer PASS; PUSH BLOCKED by GitHub push protection (fake test token); handling
- **`code-reviewer` on `ee459a7`:** Verdict PASS, no blocking issues. Notes: only `xoxb-` is exercised, not `xoxp-` (a single pattern `xox[abpr]-`, deleting it is caught). T-028-R1 is slice evidence for F005, not acceptance.
- **PUSH BLOCKED (first time tonight):** `git push origin mission/2a-foundation` was rejected by GitHub push protection (GH013): "Slack API Token" at commit `ee459a7`, `tests/test-ledger.py:262`, the fake `xoxb-…` value that T-028-R1 uses to test the privacy rule. Only that one was flagged (`ghp_…` and `AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE` were not). The last successful push was the record commit before T-028-R1 was dispatched; the two newer commits (`ee459a7` the worker's, and my record `197518e`) and everything after them are **local only**.
- **What I did not do, and why:** (1) I did **not** open the "allow this secret" URL: allowing a flagged secret on the owner's repository is the owner's decision, not part of "push this branch". (2) I did **not** rewrite history to remove the token: `ee459a7` is cited by the receipt and the VerificationRecord of run `fb246641-…`, and amending it would break the evidence chain (receipts and records bind commit hashes). (3) A later commit that removes the token would not help: the push still contains `ee459a7`. The branch is unchanged; nothing was forced.
- **Options for the owner:** (a) open the GitHub URL from the push error and allow the single fake token (it is a made-up test value; fastest; does not touch evidence); (b) approve rewriting the two unpushed commits and re-running T-028-R1 as a new run (breaks nothing already pushed, costs a run). I recommend (a).
- **Going forward (rule I applied now):** no task I write may put secret-shaped literals in source: fake secrets in tests must be **built at run time from fragments** (for example `"xox" + "b-" + "0"*12`), so scanners do not see a token in the file. T-028-R2 (below) and later tests follow this; I add it to the task text. **L-022 candidate:** a test that needs a secret-shaped value builds it at run time from fragments, because GitHub push protection scans the source.
- **The loop continues locally**: I keep committing; the push is retried after each run, so the moment the owner allows the token (or approves the rewrite) everything goes up. Work done so far is not at risk (the local repository has it; the remote has the history up to the last successful push).

  - Spawn 3: still 1 event.
  - Back to the binding: a second event, `gpt-6.1-luna → gpt-6-luna`. The comparison is with the last event, not with the binding.

- **(6) Where the drift log lives.** It is `.tasks/drift/<slug>.jsonl`, which is gitignored. Nothing in T-024 puts it into Git; T-030 folds it into `<mission>/drift.jsonl`, keyed by the sha256 of each canonical line. Until then, deleting the file loses the history: event times, intermediate models and inventory hashes. The next spawn then compares with `approved_resolution` again. If the current resolution differs from the binding, a new `from=binding` event is written. If it equals the binding, the earlier drift leaves no trace. Per-run specs still record each run's model, but they are also machine-local. Note for T-030: each event line is `json.dumps(sort_keys=True, separators=(",",":"))`, so the fold must hash exactly that form.

Text values must be non-empty `str`. Counts must be non-bool `int` ≥ 0. With no `observation`, `build_receipt` writes `observe_output.all_unknown()`.
- **(3) No spec or seat model can become `observed` — confirmed.** The only writer is `execution_runner.py:143` `write_receipt(...)`, and it passes no `observation`. The `write` CLI has no observation argument. `observe_output.observe(provider, raw)` cannot see the spec. So every new receipt is all-`unknown` until T-020.
- **(4) Receipt guards untouched — confirmed from the diff.** `_atomic_create` (no-clobber, 0400), `capture_before`, `git_delta`, the spec-hash binding, the scope recomputation, the commit/changed-path rechecks and `receipt_link` are byte-unchanged. The changes are limited to: the import, the `FIELDS_V1`/`FIELDS` split, `validate_observation`, the `observation=None` parameter, `schema_version: 2`, and a version dispatch at the top of `validate_receipt`. `execution_runner.py`, `execute_provider.py`, `verification_record.py`, `verify-mission.sh` and both launchers have no diff. The launchers snapshot only the shell script, and Python runs from live `scripts/`, so the new `observe_output` import resolves (both the manifest and the integration fixture list it).
- **(5) Mutations (scratch copy, `tests/test-execution-receipt.py`; baseline 3 tests OK; the file was restored and `cmp` shows it identical):**
  - Caught (exit 1): A v2 skips `validate_observation`; C `unknown` with a value; E negative usage; F extra observation key; G build writes v1; K default records the spec model as `observed`.
  - **Survives:** B, the `source` enum check removed. The "bad source" case uses `source: "guessed", value: null`, so the null check rejects it first. This negative is vacuous.
  - Survives, untested: H v1 receipt carrying an `observation`; I bool as usage; J non-text model.
  - Survives, equivalent: D (the type check also rejects null).
- **(6) Verifier — confirmed.** `verification_record.py` reads named receipt fields only and never the receipt's `schema_version` or `observation`. Old receipts load as in (1). New v2 receipts go through `spawn-worker.sh` → `verify-mission.sh --run-id` in `tests/test-verification-record.py`, which was command 3 (exit 0) of the passing VerificationRecord `verification/a80bf0df….json`.

  - Old schema: 76/76 real receipts valid.
  - New schema: 0/76 valid, plus a synthetic v2 all-unknown receipt invalid. For the T-019 receipt the error is `Additional properties are not allowed ('schema_version' was unexpected)` in both branches.
  - Probe in scratch: declaring `schema_version` (`enum [1,2]`) and `observation` (`$ref observation`) in `receiptBody` makes 76/76 v1 and the v2 receipt valid. It also accepts a v1 receipt that carries `observation`, so the v1 branch must also forbid that key.
  - Effect: the manifest installs this file into consumers, and T-027 reads it. Objective 3 ("update the JSON schema, `oneOf` v1/v2") is not met. Nothing in the suite validates against the schema, so this passed unnoticed.
  - Second mismatch: the schema's `nonempty` pattern rejects text with leading/trailing whitespace, but the code accepts it. Pick one rule.
- **Proposed correction, T-019-R1 (feature F003; any economy seat; Architect review):**
  - Allow-list: `schemas/execution-receipt.schema.json`, `tests/test-execution-receipt.py`, PROGRESS.md. No code change, unless the text rule is aligned in code instead, in which case add `scripts/execution_receipt.py`.

    - Negatives: `{"source":"guessed","value":"x"}` (kills B), v1 with `observation` (H), `true` as usage (I), `7` as model (J), v2 without `observation`.
  - Verification commands: T-019's seven commands, plus one that validates every UUID receipt in `$PURRFECT_LIVE_ROOT/.tasks/receipts` against the schema **when `jsonschema` is importable**, skipping with a printed notice otherwise.
- **Non-blocking notes:** (a) a non-hashable `source` (e.g. a list) raises `TypeError`, not `ReceiptError`. That still fails closed, but `isinstance(source, str)` first would give a clean error. (b) `schema_version` `true`/`2.0` compare equal to 1/2. This predates T-019 for v1; harmless today.

- **Answer to the worker's open question: the literal `.agents/` prefix rule is safe and right; do not widen it.**
  - In this glob language (`execution_receipt.glob_regex`), a pattern that starts with the literal `.agents/` can only match paths under `.agents/`, and `..`/`.` segments are refused. The flag therefore never mounts anything the scope does not already aim at.
  - Exactly `.agents` matches only a *file* named `.agents`, never anything under it, so it authorizes no `.agents/` write. No flag is correct.

That fails closed: the task fails visibly and nothing is widened. Widening the trigger to "any pattern that could match `.agents/`" would mount all of `.agents/` for every broad-scope task. That is less safe.
  - **Rule to record (not changed here):** a Codex task that must edit `.agents/` names literal `.agents/…` paths in its allow-list. After the S2 re-smoke, the Sandbox-limits row in the dispatch-worker skill should say this instead of "route to another seat".

    - → `".agents/" in pattern`. This would mount for `docs/.agents/x`.

It does **not** see ignored paths (`.tasks/`, `.claude/settings.local.json`) or `.git/config`/hooks. A seat could reach those only by an absolute path, and its prompt does not name one. The seats inherit the caller's whole environment, as every `spawn-worker.sh` dispatch already does.

    1. Run from a plain terminal, not from inside a spawned run.
    2. Command: `bash scripts/fleet-doctor.sh --smoke --mission docs/missions/20261014-2a-foundation --timeout 300 --keep --dest <new dir outside the repo>`. `--keep` preserves the clone's receipts and logs as evidence. They hold transcripts, so do not commit them; delete the dir after recording.

    4. After the run, check `ps` for leftover provider processes.
    5. Optionally leave out `cursor.*` until T-009 (plan step S2).
- **(6) Summary location and content, PASS.** The summary goes to `<live>/.tasks/smoke/<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssZ>.json`; `.tasks/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:2`), and the file is opened with exclusive create. Fields: base commit, clone path, timestamps, mission, skipped brain note, and per seat: status, reason, run id, outcome, exit code, commit, provider/model/effort/speed, clone_reset. The only transcript-derived text is the ≤300-character `reason` described in (2). No login/account output reaches it. Without `--keep`, the dest (clone, logs, `<seat>.out`) is removed.
- **Worker's `fleet-doctor.sh --help` slip:** it ran the doctor's default probe, which runs only the registry `login_check` per provider (`fleet_doctor.py:57`), not a task or model run. Whether it counts against the budget is the Front Desk's call.

  2. a fake that writes outside its allow-list, asserting `unusable`;

- **Design questions resolved (alternative rejected):**
  1. Smoke environment: a scratch `git clone` of HEAD; each seat runs `spawn-worker.sh` inside the clone, so the clone is the workspace root and the Phase 1 `--repo` guard is unchanged. Rejected: a linked worktree (its `.git` is a file, so Codex's `--add-dir <repo>/.git` would not cover the object store and Codex would fail for a reason the live tree never has); lifting `--repo` (not allowed in Phase 1). The clone's smoke fleet is a byte copy of the frozen fleet except `mission.path`, with an `approved_by` that names the source file and hash; the live snapshot is compared before and after.
  2. Smoke result has three states (`usable`, `usable-with-limit`, `unusable`), decided from receipts and Git only, so a Codex seat with the `.agents/` limit stays usable for other work. Brain seats are not smoked; the latest completed brain receipt proves them (F007 mode launches one brain run, because its audit needs it). The real smoke is an orchestrator step, never a verification command (it spends quota).
  3. Cursor fix: new provider contract 4 (plain model ID); contracts 1-3 unchanged, so v1-v3 evidence stays byte for byte. Contract 4 refuses a model name whose effort token contradicts the spec effort. Rejected: sending `grok-4.7-xhigh` under a spec that records `medium` (false evidence).
  4. Clean-worktree verifier keeps record schema v1 and exports `PURRFECT_LIVE_ROOT` for checks that need `.tasks/` state (F007's evidence). Numbered records are a separate task, so each guard change has its own review.
  5. Seat limits: only allow-list write limits are decidable by `dispatch_policy.py`; login-state limits stay a routing rule. Rejected: parsing worker-check prose.
  6. Codex `.agents/`: answered by a direct probe (P-1, with a control run) before any code; T-013 only on PASS.
  7. Carrier feature: backlog tasks T-007, T-008, T-010, T-011, T-012 are filed under F007 (dispatch needs a pending feature; F001 is accepted). They are slice evidence only; F007 is accepted only on T-038. T-009 and T-013 to T-017 are filed under F004.
  8. Request item 6 correction: the billing-class, `no_zdr` and `priority` negatives already exist (`test_registry_type_and_legacy_name_guards`). The real gaps are fields the validator never checks (effort/fast encodings, capabilities, seat specs, fallbacks); T-017 covers them. N3 is pinned by a test and a docstring, not changed.
  9. A ledger write failure never changes a run's outcome (WARN only); drift events are folded with the ledger so they reach Git ("never silently"). F006's validator is dependency-free (`jsonschema` is not installed) and refuses schema keywords it does not implement.

**Write these tasks, in this dependency order, each tagged in a table with `category` and `size` (S/M/L) and with `review` = `Architect` or `code-reviewer`:**
1. **Real seat smoke** (`fleet-doctor.sh --smoke`): for each frozen seat (see `fleet.json`: brain, cursor.premium/economy, codex.premium/economy, agy.economy, claude.premium/economy) launch one tiny real run through the real adapter: write a file under a scratch copy of `.agents/` and `docs/`, run one check, commit in the scratch copy; report pass/fail per seat; a failing seat is unusable for the mission and the reason goes to the sandbox-limit table (dispatch-worker skill and template note). External `--repo` targets are disabled in Phase 1, so decide how a scratch copy can use the real adapter without weakening a guard (for example a scratch worktree of this repository, or a scratch repository with the scripts copied in). Pick the smallest safe design; if none is safe, say so and give the alternative. Smoke runs must not touch the live tree and must not bill credits or use fast modes.
2. **Cursor model argument fix (F004 slice 1):** `scripts/execute_provider.py` must stop sending `model[effort=…,fast=…]` for Composer; use `evidence/F004-cursor-model-argument.md` (the real rejection message is verbatim there) and build a test from that message. Keep the frozen evidence (v1-v3 specs) valid byte for byte. Note which Cursor models use effort/fast in the name and which do not.
3. **Clean-worktree verifier:** checks run in a temporary git worktree at the receipt's commit, removed afterwards; a numbered new VerificationRecord is allowed per run (old records stay immutable; acceptance cites the record hash); export `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in `spawn-worker.sh` and `spawn-brain.sh`.
4. **Codex `--add-dir .agents` test:** test whether adding `--add-dir <repo>/.agents` (only when the task allow-list includes `.agents/`) lets a Codex worker write there, the same way `--add-dir .git` works. Real-run evidence goes in a file under `evidence/`.
5. **Per-seat read/write limits in the registry,** refused by `dispatch_policy.py` before launch (task allow-list or checks that hit a limit). Known limits: Codex workspace-write cannot write under `.agents/` and cannot see Claude or agy login state.
6. **Findings N1 and N3 and the missing negative tests:** N1 `spawn-worker.sh cursor … --provider agent` rejected as a mismatch because names are compared before conversion; N3 `fleet_state.py` exits at import on a registry error and adds `scripts/` to `sys.path`; add the missing type/enum negative tests in `tests/test-registry.py` (billing class, `no_zdr`, `priority`).
7. **F003, F004 (rest), F005, F006, F007 in feature order** (`features.json` is the source of the steps; F002 was removed). F007 is a real smoke through claude, codex, cursor-agent (Composer and Grok) and agy and needs a Mac session, so give its verification commands in the verifier-only form and say which parts need a human. If F004 depends on owner decision 2 (frozen-fleet amendment), it is approved (2026-10-14 Gate 0).

**Task format (every task):** the worker template, all required sections; allow-list and deny-list; JSON-argv verification commands; a "Why each check exists" section; the long-command rule in the Iteration policy; `max_attempts: 2`; frontmatter `worker:` and `invocation:` set to `any usable economy seat (the Front Desk rotates)`; if a task must avoid a seat (for example it edits `.agents/`, so not Codex while the limit stands; or it needs login state, so not Codex), say so in a `## Seat limits` section. **Standing rules to bake in:** worker checks are focused and sandbox-safe commands only; the full suite and real-machine checks are verification commands only; tests never write into the live tree (mutate a temporary copy); until task 3 lands, verification runs in the live tree, so tasks must not leave `__pycache__` behind. **Size:** cut each task so an economy worker finishes it in one session; split guard code across several tasks rather than one large one, because every guard change gets its own Architect review.

Architect (`spawn-brain.sh`, Opus): planning, and a separate review of every guard, safety-test, verification/acceptance/dispatch change or any diff I am unsure about.

Claude guard: if the Claude window nears its limit, pause Claude dispatches (Architect, Haiku) until reset. Never credits, billed usage or fast modes; a limit error never means a paid path.
- **Order:** (1) one Architect planning run writes tasks tagged with category and size: (a) "real seat smoke" (`fleet-doctor.sh --smoke`); (b) backlog: 1 clean-worktree verifier + numbered records + `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in the spawn scripts; 2 Cursor model argument fix (F004) with a test from the real rejection message in `evidence/F004-cursor-model-argument.md`; 3 Codex `--add-dir .agents` test; 4 per-seat read/write limits in the registry refused by `dispatch_policy.py` before launch; 5 findings N1/N3 and missing type/enum negative tests; (c) F003+ in feature order. (2) Smoke first, then the Cursor fix (they unlock Cursor and agy quota), then the rest in order. (3) When F004 is done, prepare the brain-seat move to agy Opus as a drift event for the owner's approval; do not apply it. (4) After the backlog, the Architect writes a short proposal to pull parallel worktree dispatch forward from 2B (proposal only).
- **Routing (data collection):** no seat preference. Rotate economy worker tasks across all seats that pass the smoke: claude.economy (Haiku), codex.economy, cursor.economy, agy.economy. Each category should reach each usable seat in turn. Exceptions: known seat limits in the sandbox-limit table, and seats that failed the smoke; record each exception. A seat's first use tonight requires a passing smoke. After every run append one line to `routing-data.jsonl`: run_id, task, category, size, seat, model, effort, attempt, outcome, failure class (none / task / seat-limit / harness / environment), duration, tokens or cost if reported. Only "task" failures count against a model.
- **Standing rules:** worker checks are focused and sandbox-safe; the full suite and real-machine checks (logins, `fleet-doctor.sh`) are verification commands only. Every task's Iteration policy has the long-command rule. Tests never write into the live tree. Until the clean-worktree verifier lands: before every `verify-mission.sh`, check for `scripts/__pycache__` and delete it if present, recording each deletion. I never commit worker code myself: preserve uncommitted worker work as a patch under `.tasks/wip/`, restore the tree, let the next attempt apply it. Worker PROGRESS narratives are not evidence (L-009). Classify every failure (task / seat-limit / harness / environment). A non-task failure does not use up an attempt; retry on another usable seat and record why. A task that fails twice for task reasons goes back to the Architect to re-split (decomposition miss). Record every seat change and task-text change here.
- **Unattended rules:** when a stop rule fires on an item, do not wait: record what failed, the evidence and a proposal here, leave the tree clean, continue with the next independent item. Stop entirely only if nothing independent is left, or something unsafe would be needed (scope bypass, protected files or the owner's shell config, credits, fast modes). Do not accept features or advance gates; when a feature is ready, prepare its acceptance command here and continue. After every run update PROGRESS.md and commit, then push `mission/2a-foundation` (that branch only; no PRs, no tags, never main). Transcripts and logs stay under `.tasks/`; commit summaries only.
- **Morning report** goes at the top of this file when I stop or at 07:00: runs used and how many ended task-done; each failure with its class; a category x seat table of done/failed (task failures only); quota used per vendor and pause durations; features ready for acceptance with exact commands; decisions needed from the owner (brain move to agy, the parallel-dispatch proposal, anything blocked).
